## Olives Farm
_farm · parish: Hunsdon (house) / detached part of Stanstead Abbots (farmyard) · grid ref TL 40807 12652 · status: active_
_Evidence status: attested_

C15 open-hall farmhouse (crown-post roof, sooted; floored + chimneyled early C17; kitchen wing later C17; early C19 brick ranges, stables and granary) at a medieval moated homestead. The parish boundary — described as ancient — runs along the access track: house in Hunsdon, farmstead in Stanstead Abbots. Grade II* (house) with Grade II stables, barn, granary and 'The Old Bungalow'. Also 'Port Horn Farm' pairing in the 1941 WARAg return.

**Etymology** — status: attested

- `Olive` (surname) — family name of 14th-century landholders

_Note: Farm-name from family, not the tree. Whether this site was Olive land in 1343 is inferred, not proven; the fine E-1343-olive-spark conveys 'a messuage' in the parish._

## Little Briggens
_farm · parish: Stanstead Abbots · grid ref TL 39364 12616 · status: renamed_
_Evidence status: attested_

Holding with three name-phases: medieval Nether Bredons; then Newgates alias Little Briggens (18th c.); Little Briggins as a working farm to the mid C20. The present house is c.1895 by Gatsby for Mr Reeve (Truman Hanbury Buxton), red brick, with listed coach house — the old farm renamed/replaced at the park's Edwardian remodelling. Shown as 'Newgate' on OS 1-inch 1st edition (user transcription, Oct 2026), between Stanstead Abbots and Hunsdon on Hunsdon Road. CAUTION (user note, Oct 2026): the OS maps also label a DIFFERENT farm 'Little Briggins' further north — a separate farm, not this Hunsdon Road site (P-little-briggens-north). Keep the two distinct. Coordinates (user, WGS84 as read off Google): 51°47'43.8"N 0°01'12.9"E — near the current Hunsdon Road between Stanstead Abbots and Hunsdon/towards Widford.

**Etymology** — status: unresolved

- `Bredons` (?) — unresolved — first element of Over/Nether Bredons; no EPNS entry found
- `Briggens` (OE?) — possibly *brycg (bridge) — proposed only

_Note: The Bredons→Briggens shift is unusual; treat any etymology as unresolved._

## Great Briggens (Over Bredons) / Briggens Park
_manor · parish: Hunsdon / Roydon · grid ref TL 41181 11149_
_Evidence status: attested_

Senior of the two Bredons messuages. Chester's house of c.1719-28; Bridgeman landscape c.1720; passed Blackmore (1740) → Phelips (1792/1824) → Gibbs (1907). Registered park/garden II. Home Farm (MHT12171) is a late C16 lobby-entrance house — oldest fabric on the demesne. 'Juicy Brook' on the 1842 tithe map is a local name for the Hunsdon Brook. OS XLVII (1799-1803) shows 'Briggen Park' immediately next to 'Brogdens Plantation' — the plantation and park labels sit together at the Great Briggens site, reinforcing Bredons→Brogdens→Briggens as one name-sequence at this location. Coordinates (user, WGS84 as read off Google): 51°46'52.7"N 0°02'51.5"E — south side of that old road; area now just called Briggens.

## Halfway House Farm
_farm · parish: Stanstead Abbots?_
_Evidence status: attested_

Paired with the NORTH Little Briggins in MAF 32/826/134/9 (occupier Findlay, owner J F Buxton Easneye, 1941-43 return 'Little Briggins and Halfway House Farm'). Fits a holding in the north of Stanstead Abbots parish (Ware side). DE/Ch/T75 (1898 Bonnington estate sale particulars) lists it as 'Halfway House Farm, Ware and Stanstead' — the holding straddled the Ware/Stanstead Abbots parish boundary. Same bundle: Spellers Farm (Hunsdon), Fillets Farm (Hunsdon & Stanstead), Nine Ashes Farm (Hunsdon & Eastwick).

## Stubby Wood

## Care Croft
_field · parish: Stanstead Abbots_
_Evidence status: attested_

Close called Care Croft, abutting on Newgate Woods, COPYHOLD of the manor of Stanstead Abbots — DE/B1630/T45 (1859-1869). Its dates match the 1859 partition of the Hunsdon estate between C. Phelips and J S Walker (DE/B1630/E4: declarations, agreement to purchase and divide, Heard valuers; E3 valuation with plan 1859; E23 draft deeds 1859-69; T9 drafts). So T45 is title evidence generated by that division: a Stanstead-Abbots copyhold close on the edge of the Newgate/Briggens holding, going with one side of the split. Etymology candidates for 'Care': OE caerra/car 'cart-load, turn of land' (plough measurement), or 'care' = burden/charge (a close carrying a rent or duty) — unresolved; do not force.

**Name variants found**

| Spelling as found | Year | Where it comes from |
|---|---|---|
| **Care Croft** | 1859 | DE/B1630/T45: Close called Care Croft, abutting on Newgate Woods, copyhold of manor of Stanstead Abbots |

**Attestations (chronological)**

*(no dated attestations recorded yet)*

## Slap Ladies / Privis Lees
_field · parish: Stanstead Abbots (tithe plots 331–332)_
_Evidence status: attested_

Field with surviving c.500ft banked causeway, 4ft above ground level; destroyed west of the Millstream by 1980s gravel working. Friend's thesis: OE slaep/hlep 'slippery/leaping place' + lad/lode 'watercourse, path' → Old English route. | 1839 award: plot 331 'Slap Ladles', plot 332 'Privis Lees' — the pairing is confirmed at tithe level.

**Etymology** — status: proposed (not proven)

- `slap/slaep/hlep` (OE) — slippery place / leaping place (Gelling pp.57)
- `lad/lode` (OE/ME) — small watercourse, drainage ditch, also path; river crossing (1194 ref)
- `Privis/Preves?` (?) — unattested in HALS catalogue search — prior's Lees? compare Prior's Lees in DE/B888/T1

_Note: Millstream is an artificial c.1300 leat — weakens 'causeway is OE' but not 'name is OE'._

**Name variants found**

| Spelling as found | Year | Where it comes from |
|---|---|---|
| **Slap Ladies** | — | Friend's note on Slap Ladies / Privis Lees causeway (citing Margaret Gelling, Place-Names in the Landscape pp.23-25, 57) |
| **Privis Lees** | — | Friend's note on Slap Ladies / Privis Lees causeway (citing Margaret Gelling, Place-Names in the Landscape pp.23-25, 57) |
| **Slap Ladles** | 1839 | Stanstead Abbots tithe award schedule 1839 — digitised transcription (plot no, name, cultivation, owner, occupier) |

**Attestations (chronological)**

- **1839** — Award plot 331 'Slap Ladles' (grass), owner Henry Streatfield, occupier Allin; plot 332 'Privis Lees' (grass), owner and occupier John Bigg. Correction: the friend's note had plot 331 as 'Cappell Lane' — the schedule gives that slot to Slap Ladles; Chapel Lane itself is not a plot name here. — events: E-1839-tithe-stanstead-abbots  _(attested)_
  Source: Stanstead Abbots tithe award schedule 1839 — digitised transcription (plot no, name, cultivation, owner, occupier)
- **1840** — Tithe plot 331 'Cappell Lane' / 332 'Privis Lees' per friend's note — verify against IR 29/15/90. 
  Source: Friend's note on Slap Ladies / Privis Lees causeway (citing Margaret Gelling, Place-Names in the Landscape pp.23-25, 57)

## Stanstead Abbots
_parish · parish: Stanstead Abbots_

The medieval vill (Abbots' Stanstead, held of Waltham Abbey);umbrella entity so fines can cite the vill itself.

- **1839** — Tithe 1839: plots named by bare cultivation/descriptor terms are NOT individualised as places — e.g. 'Middle Field' (139, 575), 'Ploughed Field' (574), 'Wood Ground Bottoms' (573), 'Mead'/'Meadow'/'The Meads', 'Orchard', 'Willows', 'Plantation', 'Garden', 'Grass', plus ~65 house/cottage/barn/malting yard descriptions (c.137 plots). Also two name-column transcription artefacts: plots 25 'Part of Ollives - Part of Ollives - Homestead' and plot 26 'Part of Ollives -' (blank name). 
  Source: Stanstead Abbots tithe award schedule 1839 — digitised transcription (plot no, name, cultivation, owner, occupier)

And after the decease of Joan the same manors and tenements shall remain to Roger Dynham , brother of the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, and the male heirs of his body, to hold of the chief lords for ever. In default of such heirs, successive remainders (1) to Charles Dynham , brother of the aforesaid Roger, and the male heirs of his body, (2) to the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, and the male heirs of his body, (3) to Oliver Dynham , brother of the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, and the male heirs of his body, (4) to the heirs of the body of the aforesaid Joan and (5) to the right heirs of the aforesaid Joan. And John More and John Meryfeld' have also granted to the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, the manors of Ilstyngton' and Notesworthy and have rendered them to him in the court, to hold to John Dynham, esquire, without impeachment of waste, of the chief lords for the life of the aforesaid Joan. And after the decease of Joan the same manors shall remain to the aforesaid Charles and the male heirs of his body, to hold of the chief lords for ever. In default of such heirs, successive remainders (1) to the aforesaid Roger and the male heirs of his body and (2) to the right heirs of the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire. And besides John More and John Meryfeld' have granted to the aforesaid Philippe the manor of Dunterton' and the advowson, excepting 1 messuage, 100 acres of land, 10 acres of meadow, 20 acres of wood and 40 acres of furze and heath in the same manor which John Sargent now holds, and have rendered them to her in the court, to hold to Philippe, of the chief lords for the life of Philippe. And after the decease of Philippe the same manor and advowson shall remain to the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, without impeachment of waste, to hold of the chief lords for the life of the aforesaid Joan. And after the decease of Joan the same manor and advowson shall remain to the aforesaid Charles and the male heirs of his body, to hold of the chief lords for ever. In default of such heirs, successive remainders (1) to the aforesaid Roger and the male heirs of his body and (2) to the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, and his heirs. And further John More and John Meryfeld' have granted to the aforesaid Joan the 1 messuage, 100 acres of land, 10 acres of meadow, 20 acres of wood and 40 acres of furze and heath above excepted in the manor of Dunterton' and have rendered them to her in the court, to hold to Joan, of the chief lords for the life of Joan. And after the decease of Joan the same tenements shall remain to the aforesaid Charles and the male heirs of his body, to hold of the chief lords for ever. In default of such heirs, successive remainders (1) to the aforesaid Roger and the male heirs of his body and (2) to the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, and his heirs · source: Curated Herts feet-of-fines abstracts 1273-1460 (in-bounds extraction, 81 records)

## Rye House / Rye Meads
_manor · parish: Stanstead Abbots / St Margarets_
_Evidence status: attested_

Rye/Rey estate lands (Raurethe/Reylegh spellings in fines 1329); VCH notes tolled causeway across Rye Meads. MANSION BUILDING: licence 7 Jul 1443 (CChR) to Sir Andrew Ogard and co. to impark 'the Isle of Rye' (site + 50a/10a/80a/16a), crenellate in stone, and free warren across Stanstead Abbots, Amwell, Hoddesdon, Ware and Widford demesnes. Brick castle — purchase £1,100, inner court 11,000 marks (contemporary). Park ~157a floodplain, probably never stocked; park field names survived, incl. one suggesting 48a converted to a warren (cf. Warren questions at Hunsdon/Easneye — Rye warren is the local archetype). Sold out of Ogard family 1559; Gatehouse survives (Grade I, scheduled), moat 90x75m.

## Easneye Park (Easeny Park on OS 1st ed)
_park/wood · parish: Stanstead Abbots / Amwell boundary (NGR approx TL 381 134)_
_Evidence status: attested_

Medieval deer park of the Abbot of Waltham, emparked 1332 (Edward III), licence in our FOF context via Waltham holdings; disparked by early C16, thence Easneye/Isneye Wood. No dwelling on early maps (absent Dury&Andrews 1766); 'Easneye Wood Park' on Bryant 1822; 'Easneye Park and Cottage' 1840 tithe, owner/occupier Henry Wilkinson, c.133a. OS 1st ed shows 'Easeny Park' as woodland next to 'Ware Lodge'. 3000-acre estate bought 1866 by Thomas Fowell Buxton; Waterhouse mansion 1867-8; lodges, dairy farm, cottages. Etymology proposed 'Island of the rivers' (Lea/Ash) — Buxton family pamphlet, not EPNS. Tumulus in woods (Buxtons planted trees to protect it); flint scatters recorded 1890s field-club walk.

| **Easneye** | — | [VCH Hertfordshire Vol. III (parishes incl. Hunsdon pp.323-332, Stanstead Abbots pp.366-373)](https://www.british-history.ac.uk/vch/herts/vol3) |
| **Easney Park and Cottage** | 1839 | Stanstead Abbots tithe award schedule 1839 — digitised transcription (plot no, name, cultivation, owner, occupier) — tithe plot 308 (Henry Wilkinson / Henry Wilkinson/wood) — schedule spelling Easney. |

## Ware Lodge (Easneye)
_building · parish: Easneye/Stanstead Abbots area_
_Evidence status: unverified_

Shown as 'Ware Lodge' on OS 1-inch 1st edition (user transcription), next to Easeny Park wood, east of Stanstead Abbots. CAUTION: the Grade II 'Ware Lodge' of the listings is a different building — lodge to Woodhall Park, Sacombe/Watton-at-Stone, c.1840 Greek-cross lodge for Abel Smith, TL 326 181, ~11 km away. The Easneye-site name likely denotes an earlier lodge/cottage on the Waltham/Wilkinson estate approach, pre-dating Waterhouse's 1866 lodges (Front Lodge 1078762, South Lodge 1049058 on Hollycross/Cappell Lane). What stood there, and why 'Ware' (warden's lodge? gate to Ware road?), is unverified — chase: Stanstead Abbots tithe 1840 plots in Easneye, HALS Buxton/DE-Bx deposit, Bryant 1822, 1839-40 tithe cottage of Henry Wilkinson. Sheet XLVII (1799-1803 survey) calls it 'Warren Lodge' — likely a warren (cony-holing) lodge of the manor/Waltham estate, not a corruption: cf. Hunsdon manor's 'Warren House' in DE/B1630/T4 (1634-1730). The later OS name 'Ware Lodge' may then be a reshaping back toward the town name.

**Attestations (chronological)**

## Mardocks (farm & mill), Wareside
_manor/farm/mill · parish: Ware (Wareside)_
_Evidence status: attested_

User's OS-1st-ed transcription 'Maltocks', guessed 'Mattocks' — resolves to MARDOCKS, the manor/estate at Wareside: 'the manor and estate of Mardocks, with Moules Farm, Widbury Hill and Grumballs Farms, Noah's Ark Farm, Newhouse and Hales on the Hills' (HALS DE/Bx summary). Mardocks Mill on the Lea: millers' wills/insurance 1790s-1856. DE/B1630/T7 'Capital messuage called Mardocks or Waters Place and 250a' 1722-1837. Plumer/Hutchinson -> Ward/Proctor/Chuck/Wilkinson -> T F Buxton 1860s. Name etymology unrecorded — possible link to 'Little Maureduppiwode' in the Stanstead Abbots boundary perambulation is speculative; treat as separate until evidence.

## Brogdens Plantation
_wood/plantation · parish: Hunsdon (near Stanstead Bury)_
_Evidence status: attested_

Shown on Sheet XLVII (surveyed 1799-1803) just next to Stanstead Bury, east towards Briggens (user transcription). STRONG candidate for the OS rendering of BRIGGENS — the medieval sequence is Bredons (Over/Nether Bredons, 13th-14th C) -> Briggens/Briggins; 'Brogdens' sits between those forms (cf. variant stems Breggen/Bricken already held on P-little-briggens). If so it pins the estate name at the Great Briggens/Stanstead Bury site 30+ years before the 1st-ed TL sheet. Verify exact position against scan; compare Bryant 1822. User confirms (Oct 2026) the plantation is 'near it' and labelled next to 'Briggen Park' on the same sheet — so the generic 'Brogdens' is being applied to ground the sheet itself names Briggens: reading as a transitional/variant spelling of Briggens is now well supported. Coordinates (user, WGS84 as read off Google): 51°47'11.0"N 0°02'36.0"E — just east of Stanstead Bury, where the old road (now replaced by the A414) ran. Same ground appears as 'Pogden's Wood' on the 1873-80 25-inch (user, Oct 2026) — the planting survived; the name ran Brogdens -> Pogden's (h-init lost). Google still shows 'Pogden's Wood' there.

**Name variants found**

| Spelling as found | Year | Where it comes from |
|---|---|---|
| **Brogdens Plantation** | — | [S-os-xlvi](https://maps.nls.uk/view/257576819) |
| **Bredons** | — | [VCH Hertfordshire Vol. III (parishes incl. Hunsdon pp.323-332, Stanstead Abbots pp.366-373)](https://www.british-history.ac.uk/vch/herts/vol3) — Over/Nether Bredons — proposed ancestor |
| **Brogdens** | — | [S-os-xlvi](https://maps.nls.uk/view/257576819) — as determinant of the Plantation |
| **Pogden's Wood** | — | [S-os-xlvi-2ed](https://maps.nls.uk/view/102343331) — same wood on OS 25-inch 1873-80 survey, same location — plantation name eroded Brogdens -> Pogden's, generic 'Plantation' -> 'Wood' |

_wood · parish: Stanstead Abbots / Ware (per sheet XLVII; exact position from user pending)_
_Evidence status: attested_

Woodland named on OS 25-inch (surveyed 1873-1880) in the Ware/Easneye area (user transcription). Personal-name determinant; NO named-holder evidence yet. Candidates considered and REJECTED as evidence: (a) PROB 11/183/570 Robert Ballard, Clerk, of Stanstead Abbots d.1640 — will read Oct 2026: bequeaths a freehold dwelling + lands in Stanstead but names NO wood and identifies no parcel by abuttals; proves a Ballard held Stanstead land c.1640, nothing more; (b) 1443 feofment fines CP 25/1/91/114 #118/#119, Thomas Ballard & wife Philippe deforciants of 'the manor called Whites' (Herts) to John Boteler & co. — gentry-size Herts Ballards 250y before OS label, no Stanstead land in the fines. Also NEGATIVE: no Ballard plot in the 1839 tithe award. Gap: 1640->1873 empty. The name-source of the wood remains OPEN.

**Name variants found**

| Spelling as found | Year | Where it comes from |
|---|---|---|
| **Ballard's Wood** | — | [S-os-xlvi-2ed](https://maps.nls.uk/view/102343331) |

**Attestations (chronological)**

- **1443** — Feofment fine (CP 25/1/91/114 #118): Thomas Ballard & wife Philippe quitclaim 'the manor called Whites' (Herts) to John Boteler, John Vampage, Edmund Brudenell et al.  _(attested)_
  Source: [Abstracts of Feet of Fines (England 1176-1509) and CP 25/2 to 1547](https://www.medievalgenealogy.org.uk/fines/index.shtml)
- **1640** — PROB 11/183/570 (read Oct 2026): Robert Ballard, Clerk, of Stanstead Abbott, will 1640, proved 14 Jul 1642 to Sir Edward Bush kt. Son/heir John; daughter Anne. No wood or named parcel in the will — NOT evidence of tenure of this wood; kept as a dated Ballard-of-Stanstead datum only.  _(inferred)_
  Source: S-tna-prob-11-183-570
- **1839** — NEGATIVE: no Ballard-named plot in the 1839 award schedule.  _(attested)_
  Source: Stanstead Abbots tithe award schedule 1839 — digitised transcription (plot no, name, cultivation, owner, occupier)
- **1881** — 'Ballard's Wood' on OS 25-inch (surveyed 1873-1880).  _(attested)_
  Source: [S-os-xlvi-2ed](https://maps.nls.uk/view/102343331)

## Ivy House Farm (formerly Curtis Garden)
_farm · parish: Stanstead Abbots (Chapel Lane / Easney Street)_
_Evidence status: attested_

Messuage on Chapel Lane (also 'Easney Street' — alternative name worth noting for Easneye access) that changed name from Curtis Garden to Ivy House Farm between 1788 and 1863 (DE/Bx/T54). The Curtis family evidently held it long enough to name it; 'Curtis Croft' in DE/Bx/T13 (Goldingtons manor, Ware side, 1756-1865) suggests wider Curtis holdings. Purchased into the Buxton estates as the capstone of the T20-53 accumulation. MAF NN/36/3 (1941-43) lists an Ivy House Farm (J Wareing Russell, Home Farm) — different parish, exclude. Chase: Curtis in Fines/post-1509 indexes; Stanstead tithe 1840 plot for Chapel Lane (which name does the apportionment use?); Easney Street on OS sheets.

**Name variants found**

| Spelling as found | Year | Where it comes from |
|---|---|---|
| **Curtis Garden** | — | S-hals-DE-Bx-T54 |
| **Ivy House Farm** | — | S-hals-DE-Bx-T54 |

**Attestations (chronological)**

- **1788** — DE/Bx/T54 (1788-1863): 'a messuage formerly called Curtis Garden, now Ivy House Farm in Chapel Lane or Easney Street, with closes called Dovehouse Mead'.  _(attested)_
  Source: S-hals-DE-Bx-T54

# People — heritage notes
_Contents: John Olyue (Olive) I, Joan Olyue (Olive), William Spark (I), Joan Spark, John Shelley, William Crowley, Robert Chester, Rev. Charles Phelips, Thomas Blackmore (I, of Covent Garden), Thomas Blackmore (II, of Briggins), Feildes family (of Stanstead Abbots), Mr Reeve, John de Louetost (Louetot), Adam de Stratton, Richard Scrope, Thomas Conyers, John de Morewell, William Rokesburgh, Nicholas Blake, John Fraunceys, Henry Melleward, John Grey, Sir Andrew Ogard (Ogart, Aagard), knight (d.1454), Spyce / Spice family (of Baas and Eastwick)_
## John Olyue (Olive) I
_landholder · floruit: 1343-1344_

Purchaser (via final concord) of a messuage, 15 acres and 4d rent in Stanstead Abbots from William Spark and Joan his wife, 1343. Probably the 'John Olive [who] obtained land in the parish [of Hunsdon] in 1344' of the listing tradition. Surname persisted locally for centuries; the farm name almost certainly derives from this family's tenure.

**Name variants found**

| Spelling as found | Year | Where it comes from |
|---|---|---|
| **John Olyue** | 1343 | [Abstracts of Feet of Fines (England 1176-1509) and CP 25/2 to 1547](https://www.medievalgenealogy.org.uk/fines/index.shtml) |
| **John Olive** | 1911 | RCHM Inventory of Hertfordshire (1911), 128a + typescript *(cited by others — not yet obtained)* (via [Historic England list entry 1101951, Olives Farm House, Hunsdon (II*)](https://historicengland.org.uk/listing/the-list/list-entry/1101951)) |

**Attestations (chronological)**

- **?** — Exact-token surname sweep: only one other Olyue in Herts before 1509 (1411, Henkesworthe, as over-seer in a fine) — the Olive name in our area is otherwise known from the farm-name tradition, not from later fines.  _(inferred)_
  Source: Feet of Fines surname sweeps: Olyue/Olive, Spark, Crowley, Blackmore, Chester, Phelips, Rokesburgh (to 1509) + place sweep CP25/2 1509-1547
- **1343** — Querent (with wife Joan) in feet of fine CP 25/1/89/79 no. 274; warranty and 20 marks paid to the deforciants. — events: E-1343-olive-spark 
  Source: [Abstracts of Feet of Fines (England 1176-1509) and CP 25/2 to 1547](https://www.medievalgenealogy.org.uk/fines/index.shtml)

In default of such heirs, successive remainders (1) to the aforesaid Roger and the male heirs of his body and (2) to the right heirs of the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire. And after the decease of Philippe the same manor and advowson shall remain to the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, without impeachment of waste, to hold of the chief lords for the life of the aforesaid Joan. And after the decease of Joan the same manor and advowson shall remain to the aforesaid Charles and the male heirs of his body, to hold of the chief lords for ever. In default of such heirs, successive remainders (1) to the aforesaid Roger and the male heirs of his body and (2) to the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, and his heirs. And further John More and John Meryfeld' have granted to the aforesaid Joan the 1 messuage, 100 acres of land, 10 acres of meadow, 20 acres of wood and 40 acres of furze and heath above excepted in the manor of Dunterton' and have rendered them to her in the court, to hold to Joan, of the chief lords for the life of Joan. And after the decease of Joan the same tenements shall remain to the aforesaid Charles and the male heirs of his body, to hold of the chief lords for ever. In default of such heirs, successive remainders (1) to the aforesaid Roger and the male heirs of his body and (2) to the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, and his heirs · source: Curated Herts feet-of-fines abstracts 1273-1460 (in-bounds extraction, 81 records)

## William Spark (I)
_vendor of 1343; named 'of Stanstead Abbots' in the fine · floruit: 1343_

Deforiant conveying (acknowledging the right of) John Olyue in 1 messuage + 15 acres at Stanstead Abbots for 20 marks. Appears with wife Joan. Whether the family continues (a 'William Spark' recurs in the family memory of the Olives Farm area) is unresolved — needs census/HALS deeds sweep.

**Name variants found**

| Spelling as found | Year | Where it comes from |
|---|---|---|
| **William Spark'** | 1343 | [Abstracts of Feet of Fines (England 1176-1509) and CP 25/2 to 1547](https://www.medievalgenealogy.org.uk/fines/index.shtml) |
| **William Spark** | 1343 | [Abstracts of Feet of Fines (England 1176-1509) and CP 25/2 to 1547](https://www.medievalgenealogy.org.uk/fines/index.shtml) — standardised form |

**Attestations (chronological)**

- **?** — Surname sweep of all Feet of Fines abstracts to 1509 finds no other Hertfordshire 'Spark' — no evidence the family persisted locally after 1343 (the modern family memory of a William Spark still needs a census/deeds route).  _(inferred)_
  Source: Feet of Fines surname sweeps: Olyue/Olive, Spark, Crowley, Blackmore, Chester, Phelips, Rokesburgh (to 1509) + place sweep CP25/2 1509-1547
- **1343** — Deforiant (with wife Joan) in feet of fine CP 25/1/89/79 no. 274; warrants to John Olyue and heirs. — events: E-1343-olive-spark 
  Source: [Abstracts of Feet of Fines (England 1176-1509) and CP 25/2 to 1547](https://www.medievalgenealogy.org.uk/fines/index.shtml)

**Transactions involving this person**

## John Shelley
_mercer of London; first known owner of Olives Farm house (d.1486) · floruit: d.1486_

Held Olives Farm of Waltham Abbey. His wife's brass is in Hunsdon church (HLHS 1979 p.33). Note a Gilbert Shelley was buried at Hunsdon 1548 (VCH).

## Robert Chester
_builder of Briggens Park; MP · floruit: d.1732_

Bought the Briggens ground from the Feildes of Stanstead Abbots c.1719-28, built the seat and enclosed the park (Salmon 1728). Left Briggens to sister Jane, then brother Henry, who sold to Thomas Blackmore 1740.

**Name variants found**

| Spelling as found | Year | Where it comes from |

**Attestations (chronological)**

- **1728** — 'Robert Chester hath within a few years built a seat in this parish, and inclosed it with a park' (Salmon, Hist. and Antiq. of Herts. 253, quoted by VCH). 
  Source: [VCH Hertfordshire Vol. III (parishes incl. Hunsdon pp.323-332, Stanstead Abbots pp.366-373)](https://www.british-history.ac.uk/vch/herts/vol3)

## Rev. Charles Phelips
_heir of Briggens (4th son of Edward Phelips of Montacute) · floruit: d.1870_

Married Mary Blackmore 1792; inherited Briggens on Thomas Blackmore's death 1824. His son Charles James Phelips (d.1903) was succeeded by nephew Gerald E. F. Phelips, who sold to Gibbs 1907.

## Thomas Blackmore (II, of Briggins)
_owner of Briggens / Newgates estate · floruit: 1763-1824_

Named proprietor on the 1781 estate plans D/EH/P14 (Briggins estate) and D/EH/P17 (Newgates alias Little Briggens). In 1803 styled 'of Briggins Park near Hoddesdon, now of Paradise Row, Chelsea' in Chancery C 211/3/B195. His daughter Mary married Rev. Charles Phelips 1792; death 1824 carried Briggens to the Phelips line.

## Sir Andrew Ogard (Ogart, Aagard), knight (d.1454)

Naturalised Dane (denization 1436); chamberlain and councillor to John, duke of Bedford, regent of France. Built Rye House on the Isle of Rye after the 1443 licence (purchase £1,100; brick inner court 11,000 marks); free warren granted across Stanstead Abbots, Amwell, Hoddesdon, Ware and Widford — i.e. his demesne spread over exactly the user's interest area, which is why 'he owned a lot of this'. Wife Margaret Clifton (d. bef.1447), d. of Sir John Clifton of Buckenham. Co-feoffees on the Rye grant were the who's-who of Bedford's war household: Fastolf, Oldhall (Speaker), Whittingham. Also acquired the manor of EASTWICK in 1447 fine (CP 25/1/91/115 #140) with the same crew — so Ogard was lord of Eastwick too. Patron of a priory (Babcwell friars, pardons 1449). Rye left the family 1559; readingtherolls names him 1437-1508 (last dates may include estate trustees). NEXT: CCR/CPR calendars and Feet of Fines 1436-1460 for the full Herts accumulation — his purchases likely stitched together parcels across Stanstead/Amwell/Ware/Widford.

## Spyce / Spice family (of Baas and Eastwick)

Richard Spyce held the manor of Baas (Broxbourne) with Hoddesdon/Amwell/Wormley/Cheshunt estates by 1398 (internal family fine, 500 marks). Roger Spyce of Eastwick appears 1412 (Blaknotle+Estwyk settlement with Ralph Hulle) and again 1447 quitting Eastwick manor to Ogard's feoffees. So the Spyces were resident lords of Eastwick for ~50 years immediately before Ogard — prime target for HALS/Wheathley estate searches if any Spyce deeds survive.

## 1343-10-13 — fine
_E-1343-olive-spark_

**Property:** 1 messuage, 15 acres of land and 4d of rent
**Parties:**
- John Olyue (Olive) I — _querent_
- Joan Olyue (Olive) — _querent_
- William Spark (I) — _deforiant_
- Joan Spark — _deforiant_
**Places:**
- Olives Farm — _property_
**Consideration:** 20 marks of silver
**Tenure:** of the chief lords (freehold)
> William and Joan, his wife, have acknowledged the tenements to be the right of John, as those which John and Joan, his wife, have of their gift, to hold to John and Joan, his wife, and the heirs of John, of the chief lords for ever. Warranty by William and Joan, his wife, for themselves and the heirs of William. For this: John and Joan, his wife, have given them 20 marks of silver.
**Source:** [Abstracts of Feet of Fines (England 1176-1509) and CP 25/2 to 1547](https://www.medievalgenealogy.org.uk/fines/index.shtml)

## 1459 — fine
_E-1459-fine-293_73-446_

**Property:** The manors of Ilstyngton' , Dunterton' and Notesworthy and the advowson of the church of Dunderton' in the county of Devon and the manors of Ethrope and Cranewyll' and 100 messuages, 400 acres of land, 60 acres of meadow, 500 acres of pasture, 40 acres of wood and 10 marks of rent in Wodesdon' , Aysshendon' , Hamme , Melkhurst , Blakegrove , Warmyston' , Codyngton' and Kymbell' in the county of Buckingham
**Parties:**
- John More — _querent_ (identity unresolved — generic medieval forename)
- John Meryfeld — _querent_ (identity unresolved — generic medieval forename)
- Joan Olyue (Olive) — _deforiant_
- Nicholas Broughton — _deforiant_ (identity unresolved — generic medieval forename)
- Philippe — _deforiant_ (identity unresolved — generic medieval forename)
**Places:**
- Hunsdon — _property_
**Consideration:** John More and John Meryfeld' have granted to Joan the manors of Ethrope and Cranewyll' and the tenements in the county of Buckingham and have rendered them to her in the court, to hold to Joan, without impeachment of waste, of the chief lords for the life of Joan. And after the decease of Joan the same manors and tenements shall remain to Roger Dynham , brother of the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, and the male heirs of his body, to hold of the chief lords for ever. In default of such heirs, successive remainders (1) to Charles Dynham , brother of the aforesaid Roger, and the male heirs of his body, (2) to the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, and the male heirs of his body, (3) to Oliver Dynham , brother of the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, and the male heirs of his body, (4) to the heirs of the body of the aforesaid Joan and (5) to the right heirs of the aforesaid Joan. And John More and John Meryfeld' have also granted to the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, the manors of Ilstyngton' and Notesworthy and have rendered them to him in the court, to hold to John Dynham, esquire, without impeachment of waste, of the chief lords for the life of the aforesaid Joan. And after the decease of Joan the same manors shall remain to the aforesaid Charles and the male heirs of his body, to hold of the chief lords for ever. In default of such heirs, successive remainders (1) to the aforesaid Roger and the male heirs of his body and (2) to the right heirs of the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire. And besides John More and John Meryfeld' have granted to the aforesaid Philippe the manor of Dunterton' and the advowson, excepting 1 messuage, 100 acres of land, 10 acres of meadow, 20 acres of wood and 40 acres of furze and heath in the same manor which John Sargent now holds, and have rendered them to her in the court, to hold to Philippe, of the chief lords for the life of Philippe. And after the decease of Philippe the same manor and advowson shall remain to the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, without impeachment of waste, to hold of the chief lords for the life of the aforesaid Joan. And after the decease of Joan the same manor and advowson shall remain to the aforesaid Charles and the male heirs of his body, to hold of the chief lords for ever. In default of such heirs, successive remainders (1) to the aforesaid Roger and the male heirs of his body and (2) to the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, and his heirs. And further John More and John Meryfeld' have granted to the aforesaid Joan the 1 messuage, 100 acres of land, 10 acres of meadow, 20 acres of wood and 40 acres of furze and heath above excepted in the manor of Dunterton' and have rendered them to her in the court, to hold to Joan, of the chief lords for the life of Joan. And after the decease of Joan the same tenements shall remain to the aforesaid Charles and the male heirs of his body, to hold of the chief lords for ever. In default of such heirs, successive remainders (1) to the aforesaid Roger and the male heirs of his body and (2) to the aforesaid John Dynham, esquire, and his heirs
**Source:** Curated Herts feet-of-fines abstracts 1273-1460 (in-bounds extraction, 81 records)
